```vein
    // ---- a real use: is this a legal identifier? ------------------------------------------------
    //
    // The rule every language has and none can express without looking at characters: a letter or `_`
    // first, then letters, digits and underscores. `words()` cannot answer it and neither can `split`.

    shape $Word { text: string, ok: bool, why: string }
    mark #Rejected
    builder Candidate { $Word   mark #Checked }
    mark #Checked

    shard Check {
        var ok: bool
        var why: string

        run once {
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "identifier check" }

            target words("count _x 9lives has-dash ok_2 Zeta") as w {
                ok = true
                why = ""

                // The first character decides whether it can start at all.
                if not (isLetter(w[0]) or w[0] == "_") {
                    ok = false
                    why = "starts with '" + w[0] + "'"
                }

                // Then every character, including the first again — cheap, and it keeps the two rules
                // separate rather than making the loop remember which position it is on.
                if ok {
                    target chars(w) as c {
                        if not isIdentChar(c) {
                            ok = false
                            why = "contains '" + c + "'"
                        }
                    }
                }

                bring Candidate(w, ok, why)
            }

            emit @Checked { }
        }
    }

    event @Checked { }

    shard Report {
        hear @Checked as c {
            target $Word #Checked as w {
                if w.Word.ok {
                    emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "  ok       " + w.Word.text }
                }
                if not w.Word.ok {
                    emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "  rejected  " + w.Word.text + "   " + w.Word.why }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    // ---- arithmetic on letters -------------------------------------------------------------------
    //
    // What ordering alone cannot do. `code` gives the number, `chr` gives it back, and everything in
    // between is ordinary integer maths — a shift cipher is one modulo.

    shard Shift {
        var out: string

        hear @Checked as c {
            out = ""
            target chars("attack at dawn") as ch {
                if isLower(ch) {
                    // ROT13. `code(ch) - code("a")` is the letter's position, +13 wraps at 26.
                    out = out + chr(code("a") + (code(ch) - code("a") + 13) % 26)
                }
                if not isLower(ch) { out = out + ch }
            }
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "rot13: " + out }
        }
    }

    // ---- case, and why it needs a function ------------------------------------------------------
    //
    // Comparison is ORDINAL, so "A" and "a" are different characters — 32 apart, and "Z" sorts before
    // "a". A case-insensitive test is `lower(x) == lower(y)`, which is one call rather than a second
    // comparison operator that quietly means something else.

    shard Casing {
        hear @Checked as c {
            if not ("Zeta" == "zeta") {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "ordinal: Zeta is not zeta" }
            }
            if lower("Zeta") == lower("zeta") {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "lowered: Zeta is zeta" }
            }
            if "Z" < "a" {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "and every capital sorts before every lowercase" }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/transform_hierarchy.vein`

**Transform hierarchy: $Position vs $World** — A platform moves +5 per tick. A crate is parented to it at offset 2, a bolt is parented to the crate at offset 0.5, and a free object is unparented. The runtime composes each chain and publishes $World, so the settled report shows each object's local and world x.  
Run: `veinc run samples/transform_hierarchy.vein --ticks 3`  
Teaches: $Parent makes $Position local · $World published by the runtime · parent chains compose parents-first · need to bring in stdlib bundles · spawn/attach/mark by hand · only the parent moves  
Difficulty 3/5 · video value: good  
Notes: In tools/check-backend.sh (3 frames). $Position, $World (stdlib/Transform.vein) and $Parent (stdlib/Core.vein) come from the standard library and are attached directly after `need`, which is RULES 17c in practice.

Real output of `veinc run samples/transform_hierarchy.vein --ticks 3`:
```text
local 15  world 15
local 2  world 17
local 0.5  world 17.5
local 7  world 7
local 20  world 20
local 2  world 22
local 0.5  world 22.5
local 7  world 7
local 25  world 25
local 2  world 27
local 0.5  world 27.5
local 7  world 7
```

```vein
// transform_hierarchy.vein — a crate rides a platform, a bolt rides the crate.
//
//   veinc run samples/transform_hierarchy.vein --ticks 3
//
// In tools/check-backend.sh, and this one is not optional. The two runtimes compose the hierarchy in
// two completely different places — `Interp.ComposeWorld` walks a name-keyed store, the C# backend
// EMITS a `__Compose` over typed structs — and if they ever disagree it will not look like a bug: the
// renderer composes correctly either way, so a crate is DRAWN exactly where it belongs and simply fails
// to collide with what it is visibly touching. A game that looks right and collides wrong is the worst
// failure this project has a name for, and a frame-for-frame diff is the only thing that catches it.
//
// THE RULE (RULES 13c). An identity carrying `*Vein.Core.Relations.$Parent` has its `$Position` read as
// LOCAL to that parent. The runtime composes the chain and publishes `$World`.
//
//   $Position  — what the PROGRAM writes. Local when parented, absolute otherwise.
//   $World     — what the RUNTIME writes, every phase, before `settled` runs. A program reads it.
//
// WHY TWO SHAPES RATHER THAN COMPOSING IN PLACE. Because a composed position only the renderer can see
// is worse than none. Three lines of any collision kit:
//
//     target $Position $Collider as a { target $Position $Collider as b { let dx = b.Position.x - … } }
//
// If a parented crate's `$Position` were local, that subtraction compares a local against an absolute.
// It does not fault, it does not warn, and nothing in the picture looks wrong. Keeping `$Position` as
// the program's own value and giving the composed one a NAME means a shard that wants world coordinates
// asks for them, and one that does not is unaffected.
//
// AND `$World` IS ON EVERY POSITIONED IDENTITY, parented or not — `free` below proves it. Publishing it
// only for children would make every kit that reads it need a fallback, and a conditional migration is
// where the silent bugs live.

bundle TransformHierarchy by you {

    need "Vein.Transform"
    need "Vein.Core"

    mark #Thing
    mark #Platform

    shard Boot {
        run once {
            // The platform starts at 10 and moves. Everything above it is expressed as an OFFSET and
            // never touched again — which is the whole point: no shard rewrites a child's position.
            let platform = spawn()
            attach $Position to platform { x: 10.0, y: 0.0, z: 0.0 }
            mark platform #Thing
            mark platform #Platform

            let crate = spawn()
            attach $Position to crate { x: 2.0, y: 0.0, z: 0.0 }
            attach $Parent to crate { of: platform }
            mark crate #Thing

            // Two deep. A chain composes parents-first, so the bolt is platform + crate + bolt.
            let bolt = spawn()
            attach $Position to bolt { x: 0.5, y: 0.0, z: 0.0 }
            attach $Parent to bolt { of: crate }
            mark bolt #Thing

            // Unparented, and it still has a `$World` — equal to its `$Position`.
            let free = spawn()
            attach $Position to free { x: 7.0, y: 0.0, z: 0.0 }
            mark free #Thing
        }
    }

    // Only the platform is ever moved. The crate and the bolt follow because of what `$Parent` MEANS,
    // not because anything writes to them.
    shard Drive {
        each tick {
            target $Position #Platform as p { p.Position.x = p.Position.x + 5.0 }
        }
    }

    shard Show {
        settled {
            target $Position $World #Thing as t {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "local " + t.Position.x + "  world " + t.World.x }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/rows_in_order.vein`

**Rows in order: spawn order rules** — Three rows arrive as events out of rank order and are brought one per event. A target inside the same handler shows the per-event commit trap, a settled listing shows query order equals spawn order (3, 1, 2), and a repeat-per-rank pass prints them sorted.  
Run: `veinc run samples/rows_in_order.vein --ticks 1`  
Teaches: query order == spawn order · commit is per event · a handler cannot see its own bring · one pass per key with repeat n as i · events as a row stream  
Difficulty 3/5 · video value: good  
Notes: STALE HEADER: lines 27-34 say "INTERPRETER ONLY … so it is not in tools/check-backend.sh", but it IS listed there ("samples/rows_in_order.vein:1"). Commit 3ae01ea, "The C# backend runs emit/hear: the first reactive sample is checked", added it. The comment on lines 38-40 is also stale. It says there is no list TYPE, so a shape or an event payload cannot declare one, yet `event @Rows { xs: list<string> }` and `shape $Bag { items: list<string> }` both pass `veinc check` and read back at run time with `target r.xs as x`, `len(...)` and `[0]`. The file itself says technique 3 (`by Shape.field`) supersedes the ByRank approach; see samples/entities_ordered.vein.

Real output of `veinc run samples/rows_in_order.vein --ticks 1`:
```text
ingesting gamma
ingesting alpha
   visible now: gamma
ingesting beta
   visible now: gamma
   visible now: alpha
rows, in the order they were brought:
   e1  gamma  (rank 3)
   e2  alpha  (rank 1)
   e3  beta  (rank 2)
ranks read 3, 1, 2 — no query sorts on `rank`. The next shard shows what does.
the same rows, ordered by rank:
   e2  alpha  (rank 1)
   e3  beta  (rank 2)
   e1  gamma  (rank 3)
```

```vein
// rows_in_order.vein — what decides the order things appear in, when they arrive from a data source.
//
// The short answer, and it is a GUARANTEE rather than an accident: a `target` query yields entities
// ascending by id, ids are handed out in `spawn` order, and `bring` spawns. So
//
//     query order  ==  spawn order  ==  the order you called `bring`
//
// on both runtimes — `EntityStore.Query` ends `.OrderBy(e => e)`, and the C# backend's `VeinWorld`
// iterates a `SortedSet<int>`.
//
// A QUERY SORTS ONLY IF YOU ASK. By default nothing consults a `rank` field — which is the trap, because
// it looks like it should work. This file stores one and prints the rows out of rank order to prove it.
//
// Three ways to get an order, in increasing directness:
//
//   1. ORDER AT THE SOURCE — `ORDER BY` in SQL, or emit rows in the order you want. Spawn order becomes
//      display order. For a served page this is also cheapest: `veinc serve` builds a fresh interpreter
//      per request, so the query runs again anyway and the database has the index.
//   2. ONE PASS PER KEY — `repeat n as i` around a query filtered to `i`. Needs the key range known, and
//      costs a scan per value. `shard ByRank` below.
//   3. `by Shape.field` ON THE QUERY — `target $Row #Row by Row.rank as r`. Added after this file was
//      written, and it is what you would reach for now; see samples/entities_ordered.vein. The two
//      techniques above are kept here because they still apply when the key is not one component field.
//
//   veinc run samples/rows_in_order.vein --ticks 1
//
// INTERPRETER ONLY. Rows arrive through `emit`/`hear`, which the C# backend deliberately skips — it
// covers the identity half (run once / each tick / settled). Emitting this prints a note per skipped
// construct rather than going quiet:
//
//     note: event @RowArrived not emitted — the C# backend covers the identity half only;
//           emit/hear stays on the interpreter (Ir/Interp.cs).
//
// so it is not in tools/check-backend.sh, for the same reason samples/entities_tree.vein is not.

bundle RowsInOrder by you {

    // A list VALUE exists (`[1,2,3]`, `xs[0]`, `len`, and `target xs as x`), but there is no list TYPE, so
    // a shape or an event payload cannot declare one — a result set cannot arrive as
    // one value. One event per row is the shape that IS expressible, and it happens to be what a
    // streaming fetch looks like anyway.
    event @RowArrived { title: string, rank: int }

    shape $Row { title: string, rank: int }
    mark #Row

    builder Row { $Row   mark #Row }

    shard Source {
        run once {
            // Standing in for a query result, deliberately NOT in rank order — because a real source
            // hands you whatever its own ordering produced.
            emit @RowArrived { title: "gamma", rank: 3 }
            emit @RowArrived { title: "alpha", rank: 1 }
            emit @RowArrived { title: "beta",  rank: 2 }
        }
    }

    shard Ingest {
        hear @RowArrived as r {
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "ingesting " + r.title }
            bring Row(r.title, r.rank)

            // THE TRAP, and it is not about ordering. Commit happens per EVENT, so this query sees every
            // row committed by EARLIER events and never the one this handler just brought. Read the
            // output below: ingesting gamma shows nothing, ingesting alpha shows gamma, ingesting beta
            // shows gamma and alpha. A count taken here would be short by one, every time.
            target $Row #Row as x {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "   visible now: " + x.Row.title }
            }
        }
    }

    // A later phase, so the commit that ended each @RowArrived has happened. `settled` carries no weight
    // here — there are no folds — but it is a phase after the events, which is what this needs.
    shard Listing {
        settled {
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "rows, in the order they were brought:" }
            target $Row #Row as r {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                    text: "   e" + Entity + "  " + r.Row.title + "  (rank " + r.Row.rank + ")"
                }
            }
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                text: "ranks read 3, 1, 2 — no query sorts on `rank`. The next shard shows what does."
            }
        }
    }

    // ---- and here is how you DO order by a field ------------------------------------------------------
    //
    // One pass per rank value, each pass a flat query filtered to that value. The query still yields
    // spawn order; running it N times in key order is what produces key order. Nothing was added to the
    // language for this — `repeat n as i` (D3) and an `if` are enough.
    //
    // The cost is honest: this is O(rows × range), one full scan per possible key. For a menu, a set of
    // columns, or priority levels — a small dense integer range — that is nothing. For a timestamp, a
    // name, or an unbounded key it is the wrong tool, and the answer there is still to order at the
    // source, where an index exists.
    shard ByRank {
        settled {
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "the same rows, ordered by rank:" }

            // `repeat n as i` counts 0…n-1, so the ranks 1…3 are `i + 1`. The range has to be KNOWN —
            // that is the real constraint on this technique, not the cost.
            repeat 3 as i {
                target $Row #Row as r {
                    if r.Row.rank == i + 1 {
                        emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                            text: "   e" + Entity + "  " + r.Row.title + "  (rank " + r.Row.rank + ")"
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/random_draw.vein`

**random(): dice, coins, loot** — Each tick prints a raw random() draw rounded to 4 places, a d6 roll and a coin flip. A run-once Loot shard stores four random rolls with a random 'kept' flag, and Show lists them every tick. The generator is seeded at 0, so the output is the same every run.  
Run: `veinc run samples/random_draw.vein --ticks 3`  
Teaches: random() built-in returning [0, 1) · scale-then-truncate idiom for integer ranges · random() < p as an expression form of chance · randomness deciding stored data · seeded, reproducible generator shared by all shards · calling a stdlib fn by its qualified path  
Difficulty 2/5 · video value: good  
Notes: In tools/check-backend.sh (3 frames). The qualified `*Vein.Math.Round.roundTo` call works without a `need` line (output confirms it). The Lexer.cs comment explains that `random` was removed from the keyword map because reserving it made `random()` unparsable. Companion to samples/entities_chance.vein.

Real output of `veinc run samples/random_draw.vein --ticks 3`:
```text
raw   0.9775
d6    2
coin  true
  roll 72
  roll 76
  roll 20
  roll 90 (kept)
raw   0.4673
d6    4
coin  true
  roll 72
  roll 76
  roll 20
  roll 90 (kept)
raw   0.9822
d6    1
coin  false
  roll 72
  roll 76
  roll 20
  roll 90 (kept)
```

```vein
// random_draw.vein — `random()` called directly, and why it is reproducible.
//
//   veinc run samples/random_draw.vein --ticks 3
//
// In tools/check-backend.sh. `entities_chance.vein` covers `chance N%`, which LOWERS to `random()` —
// so the generator was diffed only through that one construct. Called directly it had no coverage at
// all, and a direct call is what a program reaches for the moment it wants a number rather than a
// branch.
//
// ONE GENERATOR, SEEDED AT 0, ON BOTH SIDES. `Interp._rng` is `new Random(0)` and the backend emits
// `World.Random()` against the same seed. That is what makes a probabilistic program diffable at all:
// the same source and the same frame count print the same numbers every run, on both runtimes.
//
// It also means the draws below are NOT a statistical sample of anything. They are a fixed sequence
// that happens to look random, which is exactly what a test wants and exactly what a lottery does not.
// A program that needs unpredictability needs a seed from somewhere this language does not provide.
//
// AND THE ORDER OF DRAWS IS PART OF THE ANSWER. One generator serves every call in the program, so two
// shards drawing in the same frame consume consecutive values. Reordering the shards would reorder the
// numbers — which is why this file draws from ONE shard and leaves the interleaving to no one.

bundle RandomDraw by you {

    shape $Roll { value: int, kept: bool }
    mark #Rolled
    builder Roll { $Roll   mark #Rolled }

    shard Draw {
        each tick {
            // A raw draw is a float in [0, 1). Rounded for printing, because the full 17 digits are the
            // double's business and not the reader's.
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                text: "raw   " + *Vein.Math.Round.roundTo(random(), 4)
            }

            // THE IDIOM FOR A WHOLE NUMBER IN A RANGE: scale, then truncate. `int()` truncates toward
            // zero, so `int(random() * 6) + 1` is 1..6 with every face equally likely — and no face can
            // come out 7, because random() never returns 1.0.
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                text: "d6    " + (int(random() * 6.0) + 1)
            }

            // A COIN, which is `chance 50%` written as an expression instead of a statement. Worth
            // showing beside `chance` so the relationship is visible: one is sugar for the other.
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                text: "coin  " + (random() < 0.5)
            }
        }
    }

    // A draw deciding DATA rather than control flow — the thing `chance` cannot do, because `chance`
    // opens a block and this has to produce a value that gets stored.
    shard Loot {
        run once {
            repeat 4 {
                bring Roll(int(random() * 100.0), random() < 0.5)
            }
        }
    }

    shard Show {
        each tick {
            target $Roll #Rolled as r {
                if r.Roll.kept {
                    emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "  roll " + r.Roll.value + " (kept)" }
                } else {
                    emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "  roll " + r.Roll.value }
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

---

# Builders, web and UI

## `samples/site.vein`

**Site: a page assembled from fragments** — The smallest reactive website: two shards hear @Request and each emit an @Html fragment, a Kernel shard emits @Render, and a ShardView concatenates the fragments and answers with @Response.  
Run: `veinc render samples/site.vein  /`  
Teaches: bundle and publicator · shared(...) doc labels · event declaration · shard with hear ... as · emit · ShardView with an accumulator var · page assembly from fragments · declaration order is queue order  
Difficulty 2/5 · video value: core · used in lesson 20  
Notes: Works. Trace lines go to stderr; their leading glyph is byte 0xFA (code-page-437 middle dot), shown here as '·' (Git Bash prints it as a replacement char). Run with MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1 under Git Bash.

Real output of `veinc render samples/site.vein  /`:
```text
  · Shard.Header [VF:Shard:Header#1] emits @local.Site.Html
  · Shard.Body [VF:Shard:Body#2] emits @local.Site.Html
  · Shard.Kernel [VF:Shard:Kernel#3] emits @local.Site.Render
  · ShardView.Page [VF:ShardView:Page#4] emits @Response
HTTP 200
<html><body><h1>VeinScript</h1><p>Reactive page, assembled from fragments.</p></body></html>
```

```vein
// site.vein — a reactive website in IOP. A page is ASSEMBLED, not returned:
//   @Request -> content shards emit @Html fragments -> ShardView concatenates them
//   -> on @Render the ShardView emits the finished @Response page.
// Run it:  veinc render samples/site.vein  /

bundle Site {
    need "Vein.Web"

    publicator SiteApi {
        shared("An inbound HTTP request.")
        event @Request { path: string }

        shared("A fragment of HTML contributed by some shard.")
        event @Html { markup: string }

        shared("Signal that all fragments are in; assemble the page.")
        event @Render { }

        shared("The response to send back.")
        event @Response { status: int, body: string }
    }

    // Content providers: each hears a request and emits its fragment (auto origin/source).
    shard Header {
        hear @Request as req { emit @Html { markup: "<h1>VeinScript</h1>" } }
    }

    shard Body {
        hear @Request as req {
            emit @Html { markup: "<p>Reactive page, assembled from fragments.</p>" }
        }
    }

    // Kernel closes the request phase (declared last, so @Render is queued after the fragments).
    shard Kernel {
        hear @Request as req { emit @Render { } }
    }

    // The ShardView: tons of `hear`, concatenating HTML (and JS) into one page, then rendering.
    ShardView Page {
        var html: string

        hear @Html as f {
            html += f.markup
        }

        hear @Render as r {
            emit @Response {
                status: 200
                body: "<html><body>" + html + "</body></html>"
            }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/mypage.vein`

**Builders: HTML, CSS and JS elements** — Defines reusable elements as builders whose output field (markup, code or css) picks the channel, brings them (including `bring 8 Item(...)`), and assembles one HTML document with a style, body and script section.  
Run: `veinc render samples/mypage.vein /`  
Teaches: builder declaration · output field name decides the channel (markup/code/css) · bring instantiates a builder · bring with a count · three accumulators in one ShardView  
Difficulty 2/5 · video value: good  
Notes: BROKEN OUTPUT, fix before filming. The page renders an empty <style>, <body> and <script>, even though the trace shows 11 @Html, 1 @Style and 1 @Script emitted. Cause: fragment builders emit the bare stdlib channel name (src/Vein.Compiler/Ir/Lower.cs:2123 hardcodes "Html"/"Script"/"Style"). Since commit 0b4ce31 ("An event is Author.Bundle.Name"), this bundle's own `event @Html/@Script/@Style` declarations are keyed @local.App.*. The ShardView's `hear @Html` resolves to that local event, so no builder fragment reaches it. Kernel's explicit `emit @Render` does resolve locally (trace: @local.App.Render), which is why a 200 still comes back. Fix, tested on a scratch copy: delete the three lines `event @Html`, `event @Script` and `event @Style` from PageApi (deleting the whole PageApi publicator also works). The view then hears Vein.Web's events, which the file already `need`s. The page comes back with the stylesheet, the heading, the counter, the button, eight <li>row</li> items and the inc() script. Once fixed, this would be a core sample.

Real output of `veinc render samples/mypage.vein /`:
```text
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Style
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Script
  · Shard.Kernel [VF:Shard:Kernel#2] emits @local.App.Render
  · ShardView.MyPage [VF:ShardView:MyPage#3] emits @Response
HTTP 200
<!doctype html><html><head><style></style></head><body><script></script></body></html>
```

```vein
// mypage.vein — HTML + JavaScript + CSS, assembled reactively from PRE-BUILT elements.
//   `builder` defines reusable html/script/style elements.
//   `bring` instantiates them (optionally N times) — each emits its fragment.
//   `ShardView MyPage` hears the fragments and concatenates one document.
// Run:  veinc render samples/mypage.vein /
// Save: veinc render samples/mypage.vein / > page.html   (open in a browser)

bundle App {
    need "Vein.Web"

    publicator PageApi {
        event @Request  { path: string }
        event @Html     { markup: string }
        event @Script   { code: string }
        event @Style    { css: string }
        event @Render   { }
        event @Response { status: int, body: string }
    }

    // Pre-built elements: params then an output field (markup/code/css) whose value is the template.
    builder Heading    { text: string   markup = "<h1>" + text + "</h1>" }
    builder CounterView { markup = "<p>Count: <span id=\"n\">0</span></p>" }
    builder Button     { label: string  markup = "<button onclick=\"inc()\">" + label + "</button>" }
    builder Item       { label: string  markup = "<li>" + label + "</li>" }
    builder IncFn      { code = "let c=0; function inc(){ c+=1; document.getElementById('n').innerText=c; }" }
    builder BaseCss    { css = "body{font-family:sans-serif;margin:2rem} button{cursor:pointer}" }

    // A shard brings in as many elements as the page needs.
    shard Ui {
        hear @Request as req {
            bring BaseCss()
            bring Heading("Counter")
            bring CounterView()
            bring Button("+1")
            bring 8 Item("row")        // build three list items
            bring IncFn()
        }
    }

    shard Kernel {
        hear @Request as req { emit @Render { } }
    }

    // The page: hears html/script/style fragments and assembles one document.
    ShardView MyPage {
        var html: string
        var js: string
        var css: string

        hear @Html as f   { html += f.markup }
        hear @Script as s { js += s.code }
        hear @Style as st { css += st.css }

        hear @Render as r {
            emit @Response {
                status: 200
                body: "<!doctype html><html><head><style>" + css + "</style></head><body>" + html + "<script>" + js + "</script></body></html>"
            }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/shaped.vein`

**Shapes as builder parameters** — Declares element data as shapes ($Heading, $Button) and has builders include them, so the shape's fields become the builder's positional parameters at the bring site.  
Run: `veinc render samples/shaped.vein /`  
Teaches: shape declaration · $Shape include in a builder · include is compile-time field expansion · positional binding of included fields  
Difficulty 2/5 · video value: good  
Notes: BROKEN OUTPUT: the page renders `<html><body></body></html>` although the trace shows two @Html emitted. It is the same regression as mypage.vein. Builder output goes to the bare stdlib @Html (Lower.cs:2123), but the bundle declares its own @Html, which since commit 0b4ce31 is keyed @local.App.Html and is what the ShardView hears. Fix, tested on a scratch copy: delete line 11 (`event @Html { markup: string }`). The render then gives `<html><body><h1>Counter</h1><button id="inc" class="primary" onclick="inc()">+1</button></body></html>`. builder_defaults.vein and web_app cover the same concept (a shape include as a builder signature) with working output.

Real output of `veinc render samples/shaped.vein /`:
```text
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Kernel [VF:Shard:Kernel#2] emits @local.App.Render
  · ShardView.MyPage [VF:ShardView:MyPage#3] emits @Response
HTTP 200
<html><body></body></html>
```

```vein
// shaped.vein — elements modeled as SHAPES (structured data), rendered by builders that are
// given the shape VALUE. Instead of raw HTML strings scattered around, each element's data
// (id/class/label/onclick) is a typed shape you provide to the builder.
// Run:  veinc render samples/shaped.vein /

bundle App {
    need "Vein.Web"

    publicator PageApi {
        event @Request  { path: string }
        event @Html     { markup: string }
        event @Render   { }
        event @Response { status: int, body: string }

        // Element data as shapes:
        shape $Heading { text: string }
        shape $Button  { label: string, id: string, class: string, onclick: string }
    }

    // Builders INCLUDE the shape ($Heading / $Button) — its fields become the builder's params
    // directly, so there's no literal to construct and no name to repeat at the `bring` site.
    builder Heading {
        $Heading
        markup = "<h1>" + text + "</h1>"
    }
    builder Button {
        $Button
        markup = "<button id=\"" + id + "\" class=\"" + class + "\" onclick=\"" + onclick + "()\">" + label + "</button>"
    }

    // Provide the fields straight — positionally, or `bring Button ?` to see/fill them.
    shard Ui {
        hear @Request as req {
            bring Heading("Counter")
            bring Button("+1", "inc", "primary", "inc")
        }
    }

    shard Kernel {
        hear @Request as req { emit @Render { } }
    }

    ShardView MyPage {
        var html: string
        hear @Html as f { html += f.markup }
        hear @Render as r {
            emit @Response { status: 200, body: "<html><body>" + html + "</body></html>" }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/handles.vein`

**Handles: one name, three channels** — Cross-links HTML, CSS and JS through handle values (shapes $Id, $Class, $Fn). Each handle is declared once in a shard and passed to the builders for the element, the style rule and the script, so the DOM id and function name are typed once.  
Run: `veinc render samples/handles.vein /`  
Teaches: shape-typed builder parameters · shape literals (Id { name: ... }) · nested shape field access (b.id.name) · let bindings · one value feeding markup, css and code channels  
Difficulty 3/5 · video value: good  
Notes: BROKEN OUTPUT: the trace shows 1 @Style, 2 @Html and 1 @Script emitted, but the final document is empty. It is the same regression as mypage.vein. Builders emit the bare stdlib channel, while the ShardView hears the bundle's own locally declared @Html/@Script/@Style, which have been keyed @local.App.* since commit 0b4ce31. Fix, tested on a scratch copy: delete lines 11-13 (the local `event @Html`, `@Script` and `@Style`). The page then renders the .primary rule, the span, the button and `function inc(){…}`. Once fixed, note that the button is built with `id: counter`, so the span and the button BOTH get id="counter", a duplicate DOM id. inc() still works only because getElementById returns the span, which comes first. web_app's /counter is the evolved version of this idea: its ids are derived from entities, and it renders correctly.

Real output of `veinc render samples/handles.vein /`:
```text
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Style
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Ui [VF:Shard:Ui#1] emits @Script
  · Shard.Kernel [VF:Shard:Kernel#2] emits @local.App.Render
  · ShardView.MyPage [VF:ShardView:MyPage#3] emits @Response
HTTP 200
<!doctype html><html><head><style></style></head><body><script></script></body></html>
```

```vein
// handles.vein — HTML / JS / CSS cross-linked by HANDLES (id, class, function).
// A handle is a shape carrying a name (and, for a function, a body). Declare each ONCE, then hand
// the SAME handle value to the element, the script, and the style. The name can't drift, because it
// lives in one place.  Run:  veinc render samples/handles.vein /

bundle App {
    need "Vein.Web"

    publicator PageApi {
        event @Request  { path: string }
        event @Html     { markup: string }
        event @Script   { code: string }
        event @Style    { css: string }
        event @Render   { }
        event @Response { status: int, body: string }

        // Handle types (would ship in `use Web`):
        shape $Id    { name: string }
        shape $Class { name: string }
        shape $Fn    { name: string, body: string }

        // Element data references handles instead of loose strings. Named $Control, not $Button, because
        // *Vein.UI.Widgets already declares a $Button of all-string fields — components unify by NAME
        // (docs/RULES.md 15b), so two shapes so called become one with two meanings the moment both are
        // linked into an app. VS0220 says so at the declaration.
        shape $Control { id: Id, class: Class, onclick: Fn, label: string }
    }

    // Builders: `builder Name { params… ; markup/code/css = template }`. The output field's name
    // decides the kind (markup→html, code→script, css→style); required params have no `=`.
    builder Display {
        slot: Id
        initial: string
        markup = "<span id=\"" + slot.name + "\">" + initial + "</span>"
    }
    builder Button {
        b: Control
        markup = "<button id=\"" + b.id.name + "\" class=\"" + b.class.name + "\" onclick=\"" + b.onclick.name + "()\">" + b.label + "</button>"
    }
    builder Define {
        f: Fn
        code = "function " + f.name + "(){ " + f.body + " }"
    }
    builder Rule {
        c: Class
        decl: string
        css = "." + c.name + "{" + decl + "}"
    }

    shard Ui {
        hear @Request as req {
            // Declare each handle ONCE. inc's body reads counter.name, so "counter" is written once.
            let counter = Id { name: "counter" }
            let primary = Class { name: "primary" }
            let inc     = Fn { name: "inc", body: "let e=document.getElementById('" + counter.name + "'); e.innerText=(+e.innerText)+1" }

            bring Rule(primary, "cursor:pointer;font-weight:bold")
            bring Display(counter, "0")
            bring Button(Control { id: counter, class: primary, onclick: inc, label: "+1" })
            bring Define(inc)
        }
    }

    shard Kernel { hear @Request as req { emit @Render { } } }

    ShardView MyPage {
        var html: string
        var js: string
        var css: string
        hear @Html as f   { html += f.markup }
        hear @Script as s { js += s.code }
        hear @Style as st { css += st.css }
        hear @Render as r {
            emit @Response { status: 200, body: "<!doctype html><html><head><style>" + css + "</style></head><body>" + html + "<script>" + js + "</script></body></html>" }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/builder_defaults.vein`

**Builder defaults, base and ?** — One builder includes three shapes whose fields carry defaults, so `bring Panel("Welcome")` is a complete call. It shows `base` filling a slot with its declared default, and `?` (FillRest) knowingly leaving a required field empty, then prints what got built, sorted by Layout.order.  
Run: `veinc run samples/builder_defaults.vein --ticks 1`  
Teaches: default values on shape fields · several $Shape includes in one builder · positional binding across includes · only trailing defaults are optional · base keyword · ? FillRest · identity template via mark · target ... by Shape.field ordering · settled  
Difficulty 3/5 · video value: core · used in lesson 15  
Notes: Works. In tools/check-backend.sh (with 2 ticks). The `bring Panel ( "salu", base, … )` call (lines 114-122) is the editor's `?` expansion, left in while testing and kept on purpose by commit 08a2381 as a worked example of what the editor writes. Each comment names the flattened slot it fills. It makes a good on-screen signature, but its comment spacing is uneven. The second query targets only `$Style #Panel` yet reads p.Layout.column, and that works (col=1 prints for Footer). The output heading says "column/order", but only `order` drives the sort. The header comment on lines 82-83 is wrong. Swapping $Content and $Layout puts `title` at position 6, behind five defaults, not at 'position 3, behind two defaults': `veinc scaffold` on a swapped copy lists column, order, class, accent, padded, title, body. Do not read that line aloud.

Real output of `veinc run samples/builder_defaults.vein --ticks 1`:
```text
-- panels, in the column/order the layout asked for --
   Welcome
      (no description yet)
   Install
      Run veinc new, then veinc run.
   Danger
      (no description yet)
   salu
      (no description yet)
   Footer
      (no description yet)
-- the styling each one ended up with --
   class=panel  accent=#4444aa  padded=true  col=0
   class=panel  accent=#4444aa  padded=true  col=0
   class=panel warn  accent=#aa4444  padded=true  col=0
   class=panel  accent=#4444aa  padded=true  col=0
   class=panel  accent=#4444aa  padded=false  col=1
-- a `?` draft: zero where there is no default, default where there is --
   title=[Untitled] note=[] slug=[untitled]
```

```vein
// builder_defaults.vein — several shapes in one builder, and defaults so a call can leave most of it out.
//
//   veinc run samples/builder_defaults.vein --ticks 1
//
// Yes, it is possible, and the shape is where the default belongs — not the builder. A `$Shape` include
// is a compile-time field expansion (docs/RULES.md 8), so the shape's defaults come with its fields, and
// every builder that includes it inherits them. Declare `= ""` once and every call site gets shorter.
//
// THE ONE RULE THAT DECIDES YOUR LAYOUT: arguments bind POSITIONALLY, in declaration order, straight
// across include boundaries (RULES.md 9). There are no named arguments. So an argument cannot skip past
// a default to reach the parameter after it, and:
//
//     ONLY A TRAILING RUN OF DEFAULTS IS OPTIONAL.
//
// A default in the MIDDLE is not optional at all — it is a required slot that happens to have a fallback
// nobody can reach. `builder T { $A $B }` where `$A { x, y = 99 }` and `$B { z }` gives (x, y, z), and
// `bring T(1, 2)` puts 2 into `y` and leaves `z` empty. VS0228 says so, and it was written to count
// defaults rather than find the last required slot, so it missed exactly this case until this file
// caught it. Order your includes so everything optional ends up last.
//
// `base` IS HOW YOU REACH PAST ONE. Positional binding with no named arguments means a late parameter
// can only be reached by filling every slot in front of it, and before `base` that meant copying the
// declared defaults out by hand — the same value in two places, free to drift. `base` in an argument
// position means "whatever the declaration said for this one":
//
//     bring Panel("Danger", base, "panel warn", "#aa4444")
//
// It fills a slot; it does not skip one. The commas stay, and the argument count still has to match.
// On a parameter that has NO default it is VS0231 and a typed zero — `base` is a claim that a default
// exists, and when it does not, the author is holding a wrong picture of the signature.
//
// `base` stays legal as a FIELD name (`shape S { base: string }`, `c.S.base`), like `from`, `count` and
// `to` — only a bare `base` in expression position is the keyword.
//
// TWO WAYS TO SAY "FEWER ON PURPOSE", and they compose rather than compete:
//
//     a default        `body: string = "(none)"`      the DECLARATION supplies a value, once, for
//                                                     every call site that stops short of it
//     `?` (FillRest)   `bring Panel("hi", ?)`         the CALL says "fill the rest" — using each
//                                                     field's default where it has one, and a typed
//                                                     zero (0 / "" / false) where it does not
//
// So `?` is not "zeros instead of defaults" — it respects them. What `?` adds is permission: a field
// with NO default is normally required, and `?` is how a call site says it is knowingly leaving it
// empty. Without it that is VS0228; with it, nothing is reported, because you said so.
//
// The `?` goes INSIDE the argument list — `bring Panel("hi", ?)`, not `bring Panel("hi")?`.

bundle BuilderDefaults by you {

    // ---- three shapes, each with its own defaults ---------------------------------------------------
    //
    // Kept SEPARATE rather than merged into one big shape, because a shape is the thing you query
    // (RULES.md 15b). `target $Style` finds everything styled, whatever else it is — which one merged
    // $Card could never express.

    shape $Content {
        title: string
        body: string = "(no description yet)"
    }

    shape $Style {
        class: string = "panel"
        accent: string = "#4444aa"
        padded: bool = true
    }

    shape $Layout {
        column: int = 0
        order: int = 0
    }

    mark #Panel

    // The include ORDER is the parameter order, and it is chosen so the required field comes first and
    // everything optional trails it:
    //
    //     title, body, class, accent, padded, column, order
    //     ^^^^^  ^-------------------------------------------^
    //     required          all defaulted, so all skippable
    //
    // Swap `$Content` and `$Layout` here and `bring Panel("Welcome")` stops compiling cleanly: `title`
    // would sit at position 3, behind two defaults nothing can skip.
    builder Panel { $Content $Style $Layout   mark #Panel }

    // ---- calling it, four ways ----------------------------------------------------------------------

    shard Build {
        run once {
            // 1. ONE argument. The other six defaults apply — this is the payoff.
            bring Panel("Welcome")

            // 2. Two: override the body, keep the styling and layout.
            bring Panel("Install", "Run veinc new, then veinc run.")

            // 3. Down to `accent`. Binding is positional, so every slot before it must be filled — but
            //    `base` fills one with whatever the declaration said, instead of you retyping it. Read
            //    it as "the usual value here":
            bring Panel("Danger", base, "panel warn", "#aa4444")

            //    Without `base` that line is the same call with the default copied out by hand, which
            //    is a value that now exists in two places and can drift:
            //
            //        bring Panel("Danger", "(no description yet)", "panel warn", "#aa4444")
            //
            //    `base` is the answer to "I only want to change the LAST argument". It is not a way to
            //    skip a slot — the comma is still there, and the count still has to match.

            // 4. All seven, with `base` for the three that keep their declared value. This is the line
            //    that motivated the keyword: reaching `column` and `order` used to mean retyping the
            //    body, the class and the accent, none of which were changing.
            bring Panel("Footer", base, base, base, false, 1, 9)

            bring Panel (
                "salu",     // title: string   from $Content   REQUIRED — give it a value
                base,     // body: string   from $Content   optional — default "(no description yet)"
                base,     // class: string   from $Style   optional — default "panel"
                base,     // accent: string   from $Style   optional — default "#4444aa"
                base,     // padded: bool   from $Style   optional — default true
                base,     // column: int   from $Layout   optional — default 0
                base      // order: int   from $Layout   optional — default 0
            )
        }
    }

    // ---- what got built ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    shard Show {
        settled {
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "-- panels, in the column/order the layout asked for --" }

            // `by Style.class` would sort by appearance; `by Layout.order` is the one that means
            // anything here. Three shapes on one identity, and the query picks which of them to sort by.
            target $Content #Panel by Layout.order as p {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "   " + p.Content.title }
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "      " + p.Content.body }
            }

            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "-- the styling each one ended up with --" }
            target $Style #Panel by Layout.order as p {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                    text: "   class=" + p.Style.class + "  accent=" + p.Style.accent +
                          "  padded=" + p.Style.padded + "  col=" + p.Layout.column
                }
            }
        }
    }

    // ---- and the `?` form ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
    //
    // A DIFFERENT shape, so it does not mix into the query above — and deliberately one where the second
    // field has NO default, because that is the case `?` exists for.
    //
    // `note` is required: `bring Draft("Untitled")` alone would be VS0228, "note will be empty". Adding
    // `?` does not change what happens — `note` is empty either way — it changes who is responsible.
    // The warning says "you may not have meant this"; `?` answers "I did".
    //
    // And `?` still honours defaults where they exist. `slug` below has one, and the output shows it
    // filled in rather than blanked, which is why `?` is safe to reach for on a wide builder.

    shape $Draft { title: string, note: string, slug: string = "untitled" }
    mark #Draft

    builder Draft { $Draft   mark #Draft }

    shard Sketch {
        run once { bring Draft("Untitled", ?) }
    }

    shard ShowDraft {
        settled {
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "-- a `?` draft: zero where there is no default, default where there is --" }
            target $Draft #Draft as d {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                    text: "   title=[" + d.Draft.title + "] note=[" + d.Draft.note +
                          "] slug=[" + d.Draft.slug + "]"
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/builder_variants.vein`

**Builder variants with from** — Derives BigCoin and GoldCoin from a base Coin builder with `from`, fixing parameters by name so each variant takes fewer arguments, and shows that variants inherit the base's #Coin mark while GoldCoin adds #Shiny.  
Run: `veinc run samples/builder_variants.vein --ticks 2`  
Teaches: builder variants (from) · fixing parameters by name · variant chains · mark inheritance · variant adding its own mark · identity templates  
Difficulty 3/5 · video value: core · used in lesson 16  
Notes: Works. In tools/check-backend.sh. The output repeats because both settled shards run every tick and --ticks 2 was used.

Real output of `veinc run samples/builder_variants.vein --ticks 2`:
```text
coin at x=1 worth 1 goes ping.wav
coin at x=2 worth 5 goes ping.wav
coin at x=3 worth 5 goes gold.wav
shiny coins: 1
coin at x=1 worth 1 goes ping.wav
coin at x=2 worth 5 goes ping.wav
coin at x=3 worth 5 goes gold.wav
shiny coins: 1
```

```vein
// builder_variants.vein — "a big coin is a coin worth five", said once instead of at every call site.
//
//   veinc run samples/builder_variants.vein --ticks 2
//
// In tools/check-backend.sh, because a variant is a LOWERING: it flattens into the builder it
// effectively is, and if the two runtimes flattened it differently a level of two hundred coins would
// be built differently compiled than interpreted.
//
// THE PROBLEM. A builder is already a prefab — `bring Coin(3.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1, "ping.wav")` is
// instantiation by argument, which is the whole of what a prefab does. What was missing is NAMING a set
// of those arguments. The two ways to say "a coin worth five" were:
//
//   1. Type it at every call site. That is what a level of two hundred coins looks like in a diff the
//      day somebody changes the sound file.
//   2. Write a second builder repeating the shape list with a different default — which DUPLICATES the
//      definition rather than deriving from it. The day `Coin` gains a shape, the copy silently stops
//      being a coin, and nothing says so.
//
// A builder cannot call a builder: a builder body is a signature, not statements, so there is no
// expression position a `bring` could appear in. `from` is the answer instead.
//
// FIXED BY NAME, NEVER BY POSITION. Fixing "slot 4" would break the moment the base gained a shape,
// which is failure 2 above wearing a new spelling. And a fixed parameter is then not a parameter at
// all — it consumes no argument, so the remaining slots still bind positionally and in order.

bundle BuilderVariants by you {

    shape $Position { x: float, y: float, z: float }
    shape $Prize { value: int, sound: string }

    mark #Coin
    mark #Shiny

    // The base. Everything below derives from it, so a shape added HERE reaches all of them.
    builder Coin { $Position   $Prize   mark #Coin }

    // A variant fixes one parameter by name. `sound` is untouched, so `bring BigCoin(x, y, z, sound)`
    // passes four arguments where the base takes five — the fixed slot is simply not there.
    builder BigCoin from Coin { value = 5 }

    // A variant of a variant: allowed, because somebody will write one. It inherits `value = 5`,
    // fixes `sound` as well, and ADDS a mark of its own — which is what makes it a variant rather
    // than an alias.
    builder GoldCoin from BigCoin { sound = "gold.wav"   mark #Shiny }

    shard Boot {
        run once {
            bring Coin(1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1, "ping.wav")   // the base, all five
            bring BigCoin(2.0, 0.0, 0.0, "ping.wav")   // four: value is fixed
            bring GoldCoin(3.0, 0.0, 0.0)              // three: value and sound are both fixed
        }
    }

    // Every one of them is a `#Coin`, because a variant inherits the base's marks — or it would not be
    // the same kind of thing, and a shard that reacts to coins would quietly miss two thirds of them.
    shard Show {
        settled {
            target $Position $Prize #Coin as c {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                    text: "coin at x=" + c.Position.x + " worth " + c.Prize.value + " goes " + c.Prize.sound
                }
            }
        }
    }

    // …and only the one that added `#Shiny` carries it.
    shard Shine {
        settled {
            var shiny = 0
            target $Prize #Shiny as s { shiny = shiny + 1 }
            emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print { text: "shiny coins: " + shiny }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/dom_rewire.vein`

**Rewiring every button's handler** — Buttons are identities carrying an $Element and a $Handler. A shard walks every #Button each tick and re-attaches a new $Handler body without naming any button, and before/after shards show the change landing at the commit.  
Run: `veinc run samples/dom_rewire.vein --ticks 2`  
Teaches: behaviour as a component on an identity · identity template with two shapes · re-attach replaces a component · deferred structural changes · per-event commit · Entity inside a target loop · fn taking an Entity  
Difficulty 4/5 · video value: core · used in lesson 22, 23  
Notes: Works. In tools/check-backend.sh. Companion to web_app's /counter route. fnName(e) returns "h" + e, and the entity ids are 1-3 because nothing else is spawned.

Real output of `veinc run samples/dom_rewire.vein --ticks 2`:
```text
  before  h1  console.log('one')
  before  h2  console.log('two')
  before  h3  console.log('three')
  after   h1  alert('one was rewired')
  after   h2  alert('two was rewired')
  after   h3  alert('three was rewired')
  before  h1  alert('one was rewired')
  before  h2  alert('two was rewired')
  before  h3  alert('three was rewired')
  after   h1  alert('one was rewired')
  after   h2  alert('two was rewired')
  after   h3  alert('three was rewired')
```

```vein
// dom_rewire.vein — a shard cycling through elements and OVERRIDING the function attached to each.
//
// The companion to /counter in samples/web_app: there, elements and their behaviour become identities
// so no name is ever typed twice. Here is what that buys — because behaviour is a COMPONENT on an
// identity rather than characters inside a markup string, a shard can walk every button and replace it.
//
//   veinc run samples/dom_rewire.vein --ticks 2
//
// WHY A TICK LOOP AND NOT A WEB REQUEST. Structural changes commit per EVENT (Interp.Drain), so a
// component attached during one event is invisible until the next. Inside a single `hear @Request` a
// wiring shard and a rendering shard therefore cannot see each other, and `veinc serve` gives every
// request a fresh interpreter — so there is no later request to carry the change either. Ticks ARE
// successive events, which is where an override is observable at all.

bundle DomRewire by you {

    // The same two shapes /counter uses. A button carries BOTH: an element that holds its own $Handler
    // *is* its handler, so there is no second identity and nothing to point at. Overriding what a
    // button does is then re-attaching one component to the button itself.
    shape $Element { tag: string, text: string }
    shape $Handler { body: string }

    mark #Button

    // The function's name IS its identity — nothing stores a name, so nothing can disagree about one.
    fn fnName(e: Entity) return string { return "h" + e }

    // One line per button, because a `mark` member makes this an identity template:
    //   bring Btn("button", "one", "…")  ==  spawn + attach $Element + attach $Handler + mark #Button
    builder Btn { $Element $Handler   mark #Button }

    shard Seed {
        run once {
            bring Btn("button", "one",   "console.log('one')")
            bring Btn("button", "two",   "console.log('two')")
            bring Btn("button", "three", "console.log('three')")
        }
    }

    // Declared BEFORE Rewire and in the same phase, so it reads what the last frame left behind — a
    // change made this tick has not committed yet. On the first tick that is the seeded behaviour, which
    // is the only place the "before" is visible at all.
    shard Before {
        each tick {
            target $Element $Handler #Button as b {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                    text: "  before  " + fnName(Entity) + "  " + b.Handler.body
                }
            }
        }
    }

    // THE POINT. Every button, walked, and the function attached to each replaced — without naming a
    // single one of them. A page built from markup strings could not express this: the buttons would be
    // characters, not identities.
    //
    // The button's identity does not change, so `fnName` stays put and the markup that calls it never
    // needs rewriting. Only the BODY moves.
    shard Rewire {
        each tick {
            target $Element #Button as b {
                attach $Handler to b { body: "alert('" + b.Element.text + " was rewired')" }
            }
        }
    }

    // `settled` is where a reconciled value is readable, so this reports what each button carries AFTER
    // the tick's changes have committed.
    shard Show {
        settled {
            target $Element $Handler #Button as b {
                emit *Vein.Console.Io.@Print {
                    text: "  after   " + fnName(Entity) + "  " + b.Handler.body
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/web_demo.vein`

**Vein.Web by qualified path** — Builds a page from standard-library Vein.Web builders and events, reached entirely by qualified `*Vein.Web....` paths with no `need`. The app owns its own router and ShardView, because a library ships vocabulary, not shards.  
Run: `veinc render samples/web_demo.vein /`  
Teaches: qualified paths (*Author.Bundle.Publicator.member) · & sigil for builders, @ for events · using stdlib builders · library vocabulary vs app behaviour · ShardView page assembly  
Difficulty 2/5 · video value: good  
Notes: Works. It is the contrast to site.vein: the stdlib events are used rather than redeclared, which is why builder fragments DO reach the view here while mypage/shaped/handles render empty.

Real output of `veinc render samples/web_demo.vein /`:
```text
  · Shard.Demo [VF:Shard:Demo#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Demo [VF:Shard:Demo#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Demo [VF:Shard:Demo#1] emits @Html
  · Shard.Router [VF:Shard:Router#3] emits @Render
  · ShardView.Page [VF:ShardView:Page#2] emits @Response
HTTP 200
<html><body><h1>VeinScript Vein.Web</h1><p>A reusable html element library, assembled reactively.</p><button id="" class="" onclick="">Click me</button></body></html>
```

```vein
// samples/web_demo.vein — the Vein.Web builders assembled into a page, end to end.
//     veinc render samples/web_demo.vein /
//
// This used to live INSIDE stdlib/Web.vein. It moved here because a shard cannot be `shared`: any shard
// declared in a library is invisible behaviour that arrives with the import, so everyone who wanted a
// `&Button` also got a router and a page view. The library owns the vocabulary (@Http events, &Elements
// builders); this sample owns the behaviour that uses it.
//
// Everything below reaches Vein.Web by qualified path, exactly as your own app would.

bundle WebDemo by you {

    // Demo is declared before Router so its @Html fragments are queued before @Render.
    shard Demo {
        hear *Vein.Web.Http.@Request as req {
            bring *Vein.Web.Elements.&Heading("VeinScript Vein.Web")
            bring *Vein.Web.Elements.&Paragraph("A reusable html element library, assembled reactively.")
            bring *Vein.Web.Elements.&Button("Click me")
        }
    }

    // The view accumulates fragments, then emits the finished page when the render signal arrives.
    ShardView Page {
        var html: string
        hear *Vein.Web.Http.@Html as f   { html += f.markup }
        hear *Vein.Web.Http.@Render as r {
            emit *Vein.Web.Http.@Response { status: 200, body: "<html><body>" + html + "</body></html>" }
        }
    }

    // Router — turns an inbound @Request into a page @Render for its path.
    shard Router {
        hear *Vein.Web.Http.@Request as req {
            emit *Vein.Web.Http.@Render { path: req.path }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/web_site.vein`

**Routes and a form** — A multi-page site: shards emit fragments only when `r.path` matches their route, /greet renders a form on GET and reads the submitted field on POST, and an unmatched path produces no response, which the server turns into a 404.  
Run: `veinc serve samples/web_site.vein --port 8080` _(interactive — needs input, a second process, a network or a browser)_  
Teaches: routing with if r.path == ... · @Request method and body · form fields as payload fields · need widens bare names · 404 by emitting nothing · a <br> between inline links (bring JumpLine()) · Router emits @Render last  
Difficulty 3/5 · video value: core · used in lesson 6  
Notes: Interactive (serve plus a browser), so output is left empty. Verified offline with non-interactive renders (MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1): `/` gives HTTP 200 with <h1>VeinScript</h1>… and links to /about and /greet. `/greet` gives the form. `/nope` prints "(no @Response emitted for /nope)", which is the 404 path. The POST branch can be shown without a browser: `veinc render samples/web_site.vein /greet --set method=POST --set who=Ada` gives HTTP 200 with <h1>Hello Ada</h1><p>Submitted with POST, body: </p><a href="/greet">again</a>.

```vein
// samples/web_site.vein — an actual website: several pages, and a form you can submit.
//
//   veinc serve samples/web_site.vein --port 8080
//   then open http://localhost:8080/
//
// What makes this a site rather than a page is `@Request` carrying more than a path. `method` says what
// the browser asked for, `body` carries what it submitted, and a form's fields arrive as payload fields
// beside the query string — so `r.who` reads the same whether it came from `?who=x` or a POST form.
//
// Routing is `if r.path == "/about"`. Worth knowing: that could not have worked before, because `==`
// compared any two non-numeric strings as equal, so every path matched the first branch. See
// docs/RUNTIME.md §4.3.4.
//
// The page is ASSEMBLED reactively: shards emit @Html fragments and a ShardView concatenates them. That
// is the same pattern as samples/site.vein — routing decides WHICH fragments get emitted.

bundle WebSite by you {

    need "Vein.Web"                          // &Heading / &Paragraph / &Link — bare, via `need`

    // ---- pages ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    shard Home {
        hear @Request as r {
            if r.path == "/" {
                bring Heading("VeinScript")
                bring Paragraph("A site assembled from events.")
                // Links are inline elements, so they sit side by side without something between them.
                // `bring JumpLine()` is a `<br>`; `bring 2 JumpLine()` would be two.
                bring Link("/about", "about")
                bring JumpLine()
                bring Link("/greet", "say hello")
            }
        }
    }

    shard About {
        hear @Request as r {
            if r.path == "/about" {
                bring Heading("About")
                bring Paragraph("Every page here is a set of fragments emitted by shards.")
                bring Link("/", "home")
            }
        }
    }

    // ---- a form, which is the part a path alone cannot do -----------------------------------------

    shard Greet {
        hear @Request as r {
            if r.path == "/greet" {
                // GET renders the form; POST receives it. Same path, two methods — the distinction the
                // old single-field @Request could not express.
                if r.method == "GET" {
                    bring Heading("Say hello")
                    // One line: a newline ENDS a statement in VeinScript, so a `+` continuation on the
                    // next line is not a continuation at all.
                    emit @Html { markup: "<form method=\"post\" action=\"/greet\"><input name=\"who\" placeholder=\"your name\"><button>send</button></form>" }
                    bring Link("/", "home")
                }

                if r.method == "POST" {
                    // `who` is a form field, and it arrives as an ordinary payload field. `?` on the
                    // event declaration is what lets a field the program never declared be read here.
                    bring Heading("Hello " + r.who)
                    bring Paragraph("Submitted with " + r.method + ", body: " + r.body)
                    bring Link("/greet", "again")
                }
            }
        }
    }

    // ---- assembly ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    // The trigger, and it has to be a SEPARATE event rather than the view answering @Request directly.
    // `bring Heading(…)` does not write markup, it QUEUES an @Html event, and the queue does not drain
    // until every @Request handler has finished. A view that answered on @Request would therefore always
    // see an empty page. Emitting @Render here puts it in the queue behind the fragments, so by the time
    // it is dequeued they have all arrived. Declared after the page shards so it queues last.
    shard Router {
        hear @Request as r { emit @Render { path: r.path } }
    }

    ShardView Page {
        var html: string

        hear @Html as h { html += h.markup }

        hear @Render as v {
            // No fragments means nothing matched the path. Staying silent lets the server answer 404,
            // which is what an unrouted URL is — better than 200 with an empty body.
            if not (html == "") {
                emit @Response {
                    status: 200
                    body: "<!doctype html><html><head><meta charset=\"utf-8\"><title>VeinScript</title></head><body>" + html + "</body></html>"
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

## `samples/web_app`

**Full web app: four routes, three channels** — The showcase site: a Vein.WebTheme stylesheet, a nav rendered by a query inside an SF, a home grid rendered from #Featured identities, a /counter page whose buttons and JS handlers are identities with ids derived from the entity, a form, and a /docs reference merged in from a shards/ fragment file.  
Run: `veinc serve samples/web_app/web_app.vein --port 8080` _(interactive — needs input, a second process, a network or a browser)_  
Teaches: three channels together (markup/css/code) · multi-file bundle (shards/ fragment merges before main file) · identity templates seeding a world (bring Featured, NavItem, Btn, Readout) · bring ... as name · SF runs inline (navBar) · target query inside hear renders a page · ids derived from Entity (domId/fnName) · *Vein.Web.Js composition (addNumber, text, everyMs, setStyleExpr, raw) · 404 by silence · Kernel position is load-bearing  
Difficulty 5/5 · video value: core · used in lesson 23  
Notes: Interactive (serve plus a browser), so output is left empty. HEADER PATH IS STALE: line 3 says `veinc serve samples/web_app.vein --port 8080`, but samples/web_app.vein does not exist. The `run` field above uses the real path. Verified offline with `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1 veinc render samples/web_app/web_app.vein <route>`. `/` gives HTTP 200 with the nav (home bolded), a hero and FIVE cards. `/counter` gives buttons id='e31'..'e33' with onclick h31()..h33() and readout e30. The ids start at 30 because ReferenceIndex (20 sections, from the merged fragment) runs before Catalog (9 identities). `/docs` gives a ~138 KB page. `/nope` prints "(no @Response emitted for /nope)". Recent hand edits disagree with the comments in two places. There is a fifth card ("where are we " / "LOST IN THE FRAGMENt OF THE WORLD") while the text still says "These four cards". The +1/-1 buttons actually add ±2 (addNumber(domId(out), 2)). Stale comment at lines 77-79: it says a nested target "silently reads empty from the outer binding" and cites RULES 12c, but that bug has since been fixed, and RULES 12c now calls the workaround (an Entity plus a fn to look it up) "a choice rather than a requirement". The line 268 comment says "Three builders, four brings", but only Readout and Btn are used there. shards/Reference.vein is GENERATED (node tools/gen-reference.js): 2579 lines and ~1340 brings (ListItem, Paragraph, CodeBlock and similar), transcribed verbatim from a 2026-08-29 snapshot that contains outdated syntax (`use Vein.Web`, `end`, push buffers). The page flags this in a callout, so don't show its embedded examples as current syntax. There is no vein.discovery file or bundles/ folder.

```vein
// samples/web_app.vein — a FULL site: five routes, a real stylesheet, working JavaScript, and a form.
//
//   veinc serve samples/web_app.vein --port 8080
//   then open http://localhost:8080/
//
// This exists to stress the three channels together. A builder's OUTPUT FIELD decides which event it
// emits, and that is the whole mechanism:
//
//     markup = …   →  @Html      (the page)
//     css    = …   →  @Style     (the stylesheet)
//     code   = …   →  @Script    (the behaviour)
//
// Three streams, three accumulators on the ShardView, one document at the end. Nothing here writes a
// page; every shard contributes a fragment and the view decides where each kind lands — CSS in <head>,
// scripts before </body>.
//
// Two things the language makes you do differently from a template engine:
//   * a newline ends a statement, so a `+` must end the line it continues from — trailing `+` keeps
//     the expression open, a leading `+` on the next line starts a new statement and fails;
//   * there is no `!=`; inequality is `not (a == b)`.
//
// Compare samples/web_site.vein, which is the same idea with three routes and no styling.

bundle WebApp by you {

    // Two bundles, and the split between them is the point. Vein.Web is the machinery: the channels,
    // and the primitives to write on them. Vein.WebTheme is the look — palettes, foundation,
    // components — so this file selects a style instead of transcribing one.
    need "Vein.Web"
    need "Vein.WebTheme"

    // ---- page furniture, which is this SITE and not the library ------------------------------------
    // A hero and a card are still decisions this page makes: the theme styles `.card`, but what goes
    // in one belongs here.
    //
    // Each one is a SHAPE plus a builder that includes it. The include costs nothing — it is a
    // compile-time field expansion, so `bring Card("a", "b")` reads exactly as it did with loose
    // params — but the same declaration is now attachable, which is what lets the home grid below be
    // a `target` query instead of four hard-coded lines.

    shape $Card   { title: string, body: string }
    shape $Hero   { title: string, sub: string }
    shape $Footer { note: string }

    // ---- the browser half, as identities ------------------------------------------------------------
    //
    // /counter used to be six literal strings: three <button onclick='inc()'> and three
    // Define("inc", "…getElementById('n')…"). The id 'n' appeared FOUR times and the name 'inc' twice,
    // and nothing checked either — they are characters inside a string, invisible to the compiler and
    // to every shard.
    //
    // As identities the names stop being typed at all. A DOM id is DERIVED from the entity (`domId`
    // below), so there is no literal to keep in sync, and a button is something a shard can walk.

    // One shape for every element, not one per tag. `target` binds a single shape and cannot ask an
    // entity what else it carries, so a page rebuilt by query has to be uniform — the same constraint
    // that keeps /docs a literal sequence. `tag` carries the difference instead.
    shape $Element { tag: string, cls: string, text: string }

    // A JavaScript function as an identity. It carries only a BODY: the function's name is derived from
    // the identity itself (`fnName` below), so the thing that used to be written twice — once in
    // `Define("inc", …)` and once in `onclick='inc()'` — is now written zero times.
    shape $Handler { body: string }

    mark #Screen    // an element that belongs to the counter page, rendered in spawn order
    mark #Button

    // Declaring ANY mark opts this bundle into VS0218, so the two that were already in use have to be
    // declared as well. That is the check working: they were only ever spelled at their use sites.
    mark #Featured  // a card the home grid shows
    mark #Doc       // a /docs section (used by shards/Reference.vein, which merges into this bundle)
    mark #Nav       // one link in the site nav

    // The names the browser sees, both DERIVED from the identity — so they exist in exactly one place
    // each, and a rename moves every reference with it because there are no other references.
    //
    // Deriving both (rather than storing names) is what keeps every render loop FLAT — see RULES.md 12c,
    // where a nested `target` silently reads empty from the outer binding. A button IS its handler, so
    // naming its function is `fnName(Entity)` — no lookup, no second loop, nothing that can disagree.
    fn domId(e: Entity) return string { return "e" + e }
    fn fnName(e: Entity) return string { return "h" + e }

    // One section of the reference at /docs. The BODY of that page cannot be a query — `target` binds
    // one shape per loop and there is no way to ask an entity which shape it carries, so a document
    // that interleaves headings, prose, lists and code in reading order stays an ordered sequence.
    // Its sections can be identities though, and that is enough to make the contents list a query and
    // to let a route filter them. Declared here, in the main file, because a `shards/` fragment may
    // hold only behaviour — a shape there is VS0321.
    shape $Section { title: string, ord: int }

    // ---- the nav: a query, not a literal -------------------------------------------------------------
    //
    // It used to be one builder emitting a fixed `<nav>` with four hard-coded <a> tags, so adding a
    // route meant editing markup — and the nav could not know which page it was on, because a builder
    // sees only its own parameters.
    //
    // As identities the links are data: `bring NavItem(…)` adds one, and the SF below renders whatever
    // the world holds. `path` is compared per item, which is what makes the current page bold.
    shape $NavItem { href: string, label: string }

    builder NavItem { $NavItem   mark #Nav }

    // An SF, not a builder, and the reason is ordering. A builder cannot hold statements, so it cannot
    // run a query; a shard hearing its own event would drain AFTER the route's fragments and land the
    // nav at the bottom of the page. An SF runs INLINE at the call site, so its `bring`s queue exactly
    // where `bring Nav()` used to — first.
    //
    // It also has to stay opt-in per route: an unrouted path emits no @Html at all, and that is how the
    // view tells a 404 from a page. A shard on `hear @Request` would give every 404 a nav and a 200.
    SF navBar(path: string) {
        bring Open("nav")
        target $NavItem #Nav as i {
            // The current page is not a link to itself.
            if path == i.NavItem.href {
                bring Raw("<b>" + i.NavItem.label + "</b>")
            } else {
                bring Link(i.NavItem.href, i.NavItem.label)
            }
        }
        bring Raw("<span class='clock' id='clk' style='margin-left:auto'></span>")
        bring Raw("<button onclick='theme()'>theme</button>")
        bring Close("nav")
    }

    builder Card    { $Card     markup = "<div class='card'><h3>" + title + "</h3><p>" + body + "</p></div>" }
    builder Hero    { $Hero     markup = "<header class='hero'><h1>" + title + "</h1><p>" + sub + "</p></header>" }
    builder Footer  { $Footer   markup = "<footer>" + note + "</footer>" }

    // ---- identity templates: the same builders, building things instead of markup -------------------
    //
    // A `mark` member is the discriminator. It makes `bring Featured("a", "b")` mean exactly
    //
    //     let e = spawn()   attach $Card to e { title: "a", body: "b" }   mark e #Featured
    //
    // so seeding a world is one line per thing rather than three. `&Card` above and `&Featured` here
    // include the SAME `$Card`: one shape, rendered by one and attached by the other, which is what
    // makes the home grid a query rather than four hard-coded lines.
    //
    // Note a builder does ONE of these — a `mark` member short-circuits before the output channel is
    // considered — so `&Card` cannot both attach and render, and the two names are two builders.
    builder Featured { $Card   mark #Featured }

    // A button carries BOTH shapes, and that is what removes the last back-reference: an element that
    // holds its own `$Handler` *is* its handler, so there is no second identity to point at and nothing
    // to hand back out of a `bring`. `fnName(Entity)` names the same function from the button's markup
    // and from the @Script fragment, because both are the same identity.
    //
    // Arguments bind positionally ACROSS includes, in declaration order: (tag, cls, text, body).
    // `veinc scaffold samples/web_app/web_app.vein '&Btn'` prints the slots with the shape each came from.
    builder Btn { $Element $Handler   mark #Screen #Button }

    // The readout: an element with no behaviour of its own. `as out` names the identity it builds, so
    // the handler bodies below can address it — without that, `bring` spawns into a local the program
    // never sees and there would be nothing to pass to `domId`.
    builder Readout { $Element   mark #Screen }

    // ---- the stylesheet ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // Emitted on EVERY request, before any page decides what it contains. A 404 still collects the CSS
    // and still answers 404, because the view keys "is there a page" off the HTML, not off the styles.

    shard Styles {
        hear @Request as r {
            // The palette comes first and is the only line that names a colour. Every component below
            // reads var(--brand), var(--card), var(--line) — which is why swapping this one `bring`
            // for `Ink()` restyles the whole site, and why the dark theme is a class swap rather than
            // a parallel rule for each element.
            bring Slate()
            bring Reset()

            // One `bring` per feature the site actually uses. A page with no cards would not ship the
            // card rules.
            bring NavBar()
            bring HeroArea()
            bring Cards()
            bring Controls()
            bring Prose()
            bring Layout()

            // Long-form document styling, for /docs. Selecting them here rather than in the Reference
            // shard keeps every style decision in one place — and costs nothing on the other routes,
            // since a rule whose selector matches nothing does nothing.
            bring Article()
            bring Code()
            bring Callout()

            bring Motion()

            // &Rule stays reachable for what the theme has no opinion about — this counter's readout
            // is particular to one page of one site.
            bring Rule(".count", "font-size:4rem;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:-.04em;color:var(--brand);margin:1.5rem 0;font-variant-numeric:tabular-nums")
        }
    }
```
